European hours on the subject: 'For peace in Europe: Peaceful conflict resolution on the example of South Tyrol – 30 years of dispute resolution, 50 years Second autonomous statute” I should like to welcome Members to the European Parliament quite warmly in our midst, and I also welcome the Federal Minister for the EU and the Constitution. ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-14-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e11537	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Appreciated visitors to the gallery and in front of the TV screens! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! ‘For peace in Europe’ means: Yes, we are spending an hour in Europe today on the peaceful “conflict resolution on the example of South Tyrol – 30 years of dispute resolution, 50 years of Second Autonomous Statute”. South Tyrol is anchored in this Parliament with the South Tyrol Subcommittee, in which we are discussing South Tyrol issues on the basis of the autonomy report. Ladies and gentlemen, especially at this time, peace is not a matter of course. We are currently experiencing this on European soil, and the images we see daily make us speechless and affected. In the European Union, 447 million people currently live and 10 percent of them live in another country, i.e. about 45 million, as a minority. Minorities in Europe: Europe is a continent with minorities, and the values that are enshrined in the European Union and which the European Union gives us are also very clear. These are respect for human dignity, freedom, democracy, equality, the rule of law and respect for human rights, including the rights of persons belonging to minorities. In the example of South Tyrol – and I should like to take a look at history, he should serve the future – one should show how to shape it with minorities in Europe. On 10.9.1919, South Tyrol was separated, in the peace treaty of Saint-Germain, Tyrol was divided and at the Brenner there was an illegal border. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Worm.]] South Tyrol has been with Austria for 500 years and has now been separated for 100 years, and there are, of course, challenges for the country of South Tyrol. In 1922 Mussolini became active in South Tyrol, he wanted to make Italians violently from South Tyrol, and this naturally led to conflicts. The name Tyrol was abolished, German schools were banned, surnames and local names adapted. The South Tyroleans became a minority in Italy. There were, of course, ongoing conflicts, and in 1939 there was the so-called option in which the South Tyroleans could decide to stay or move away, which brought further suffering to South Tyrol with the Second World War. In spite of efforts, South Tyrol remained with Italy even after the Second World War. The Gruber-De-Gasperi Agreement was concluded in 1946. It was intended as a basis for a protection function, but the much praised autonomy and minority rights were not taken seriously. In 1957, a large rally – “Los von Trent” – took place. In 1960 it was Bruno Kreisky, who presented the South Tyrol theme to the UN and thus put it in an international window. However, the displeasure grew, and in 1961, in the so-called fire night, it sparkled with bombings throughout South Tyrol to draw attention to the oppression of the world. There were then processes. In 1972, the second autonomous statute – which is the occasion for this current European hour – entered into force. Certain privileges for the Italian language group fell, there was self-government for South Tyrol, and this had to be further expanded to overcome the minority conflicts. However, the final breakthrough did not succeed until 1992: Today it is 30 years of dispute resolution between Austria and Italy before the UN. The Foreign Minister of that time, with the active support of Parliament, succeeded in resolving this dispute. In 1995, Austria joined the EU and, with 1.4.1998, the borders fell. So it has improved a lot for South Tyrol, ladies and gentlemen. However, despite positive developments, we should not only rejoice, but also think ahead: We must continue working together on the autonomy of South Tyrol, developing and optimizing it. It is a ongoing process, and we also need ongoing political activities. At present, it is probably true that European laws have lost autonomous rights through case law, through centralisation. At present, it is a desire in South Tyrol – and we should take it seriously here in the Austrian Parliament and the Subcommittee – to regain autonomy rights and adapt certain things. This is about modern self-government – there was a financial arrangement – but also about economic scope. The Europe region of Tyrol-South Tyrol-Trentino is a chance for South Tyrol and for us. With the Brenner Base Tunnel we also want to set accents in the transport area. There are many, many special concerns about South Tyrol, which are of course known to those of you who are members of the South Tyrol Subcommittee, such as the fascist relics and the toponomastics. However, I would also like to use today's current European hour to thank the Italian President Mattarella for the grace of the freedom fighter Heinrich Oberleitner. However, my wish would be for the other freedom fighters to be welcomed as well. My thanks also apply to those people who are willing to work for South Tyrol: to the clubs – association of shooters, music clubs, cultural associations – the communities, the community partnerships with South Tyrol, and to all pioneers and patriots. A special thanks also apply to all the South Tyrol associations, which – you might not believe it at all – are active throughout Austria – in Tyrol, Graz, Linz, everywhere, focus on the topic of South Tyrol and thus maintain the ties to the home country. [[Congratulation with ÖVP and Greens as well as the Abg. Stefan.]] The South Tyrol autonomy is a commitment for us in this Parliament, and we want to meet this commitment. South Tyrol autonomy is also a topic for Europe. Arno Kompatscher recently made the statement: South Tyrol is “a small Europe in Europe”. – South Tyrol is a functioning European model at a small level, and so, I believe, we should build this European region together in addition to all other activities. Solving minority conflicts requires daily use, needs insight, understanding and wide vision. As the South Tyrol conflict was resolved, it is not a matter of course, and perhaps we can take an example of South Tyrol right now. When we look at the war scene, the crisis spots and the human rights violations that are currently triggered by the war confusion, I believe we need to look at peace. There will only be peace if there is success in resolving conflicts. We thank, in particular, those people who have contributed to making this dispute settlement possible. We thank everyone, including the political representatives. Ladies and gentlemen! South Tyrol is fine, but South Tyrol must also be accompanied by us in the future. Austria will assist South Tyrol in its role as a protective force in the future, South Tyrol can rely on us. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-14-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e13895	We'll get to the 1st. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. I welcome the Minister for Foreign Affairs and give the floor to Mrs Grünberg. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-14-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e13922	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! When you become a mother or father, a lot of things change in life: Daily life needs to be redesigned, priorities shift, and new challenges are to be overcome every day. When you become parents of a child with disabilities, you usually have additional special challenges. Support and promotion of the development of the child are often very time-consuming depending on the nature and extent of the disability. In addition, there are often authorities, requests to be filled out, and also visits by experts to draw up opinions so that support can also be obtained. Families with children with disabilities are constantly faced with financial and bureaucratic challenges. In order to get some work from these families, we have a motion for a resolution today: The procedure for obtaining increased family support for minor applicants should be simplified and accelerated. We thus relieve families with children with disabilities. Now, just a moment ago, to explain: In addition to the general family allowance, an increase is granted for significantly disabled children, the so-called increased family allowance. The condition for this is either a degree of disability of at least 50 percent or an incapacity to work. In order to prove the disability, an expert doctor or a competent doctor must be examined, and this doctor must then draw up an opinion. However, it is now the case that minor disabled persons currently have to provide additional proof of the Social Ministry service for the increased family allowance, although the conditions for the entitlement to increased family allowances and for the issue of a disability passport are identical. In the future, however, there will be no more, because we relieve families with children with disabilities. Already in the government programme, we agreed that there must be improvements for these families. The present proposal will make the procedure for granting increased family support easier and faster for minor applicants and will also avoid unnecessary bureaucracy. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] As mentioned above, families with children with disabilities usually have a greater effort in everyday life. That is why it is all the more important to create reliefs for these families. But this is not the only measure we are taking to support families with children with disabilities, because the already announced care reform also brings improvements to these families. In future, the increased family allowance will no longer be charged for the care allowance. 45 000 people who receive 60 euros per month benefit from this. In addition, the right to care leave is extended from one month to three months, and from 2023 a family bonus of 1,500 euros will be created for the person doing the main care at home. There are also improvements in the provision of replacement care, and there will be a right to financial support for replacement care after three days for caregivers in the future. So far, this has usually been the case only from seven days. Many measures are therefore being taken by this Federal Government to financially support families with children with disabilities, but also to reduce their work. We relieve families with children with disabilities.

Although this assessment of the degree of disability has already taken place, parents, when applying for the increased family allowance, must at present provide a further confirmation of the degree of disability. This confirmation is issued by the Federal Ministry of Social Affairs on the basis of an opinion. With this confirmation, parents can then apply to the Financial Office for increased family allowances. For families, this can mean – and in most cases it is – to make an extra appointment at the Social Ministry Service, to seek out, to send findings, to coordinate everything, and to realize the date, which means that you have to travel to the respective capital. ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-14-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e16564	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Appreciate Seers and Advisors! The rights of minorities in Austria, in particular the Slovenian minority, are enshrined in the Vienna State Treaty. In 1955 we signed this State Treaty to restore an independent and democratic Austria. Slovenian as an official language, in addition to the German language, is thus a constitutionally guaranteed law and part of the historical restoration of Austria as a free state. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP as well as the Abg. Yılmaz.]] This is about the constitutionally guaranteed right, namely the right to the Slovenian language, the right to be informed in Slovenian, and also the right to use Slovenian in court. The aim of the reform, which is being discussed here, is therefore nothing but to implement Austria's constitutional obligations so that the bilinguality and thus the rights of the peoples in Carinthia are sustained and sustained. Yes, we have this in our government programme. As a government, we have clearly agreed in our government programme that we naturally want to promote people's group rights in Carinthia and to secure and strengthen bilingual jurisdiction in Carinthia. In order to implement this government programme, we must, of course, deal with the current situation and consider what the bilingual jurisdiction is all about today. Currently, the use of Slovenian is planned in a few smaller district courts, according to the Volksgruppengesetz. And yes, we want to continue to strengthen this, with the involvement of all parties involved and also in accordance with the government programme, that Slovenia can also be used in the courts. In the interests of bilingual jurisdiction in Carinthia and in order to find a comprehensive solution, I therefore set up last year, as you have said, a working group led by the head of the presidential section of my house and, of course, together with the three major Slovenian groups. Informatively, the country of Carinthia is also involved by the State Department. And yes, there are also objectives for this working group. It is a matter of respecting, safeguarding and promoting people's rights in accordance with constitutional requirements and ensuring the effective use of popular languages in courts. It is a matter of anchoring a judicial organisation that fully ensures the constitutionally guaranteed bilinguality in Carinthia. It is also about creating the necessary legal and factual framework to effectively and functionally implement bilingual jurisdiction in Carinthia. I have already mentioned this – and that is what I have already mentioned in my reply to the question – on 21 December. ==References== The first talks were held within this working group. These have primarily served to explain the mutual positions, to formulate common objectives and to outline possible solutions. I was told that this was quite constructive. On the basis of these discussions, a first working document was prepared, a so-called first working paper, which is now to be discussed with all the parties involved. We are therefore in the middle of the discussion process, it is far from finished and it is not a final draft at all. The Federal Ministry of Justice, with the involvement of the representatives of the groups, will, of course, also involve all the parties involved and thus, together with all, promote a progressive reform. [[Abg. Kickl: Progress is the most important!]] It is important for me to note that I am seeking a comprehensive solution which is approved by the parties to ensure that the bilingual jurisdiction, which is established by constitutional law, is permanently and sustainable in Carinthia. I have a central concern to discuss and discuss the challenges in an open dialogue with all stakeholders and to consider the different fores and resistances. However, regardless of the outcome of the discussion, I would like to stress one thing that is particularly important to me: In rural areas, of course, there must not be any reductions, so that people no longer have access to the right. For one thing is particularly important to me, namely, in rural areas too, to ensure that everyone has effective access to the law and a full citizen service. This will continue to be ensured by the court days. – Thank you very much.

Easy: Mr. Colleague, you are not a Carinthian!]] This is a general question of the legal structure, in particular the closure of small district courts, which has already been strongly and often discussed and discussed in the media in this House. The situation is known: The court structure we have in Austria is relatively well known. It dates back to the Maria-Theresian era, with the requirement that every citizen of the then empire must reach a district court within a day's journey with the ox carnage. These times have long passed, the court structure is in a dynamic change. We have electronic access to the court today, we have clearly experienced in the two years of the pandemic that it is possible to obtain legal protection without personal presence or to participate in the procedures for granting legal protection. So I think that the question of whether these small dishes are still durable will also be considered in detail. The second problem with this specific question is that these are district courts in the bilingual area of Carinthia, which seem to be closed. I have followed the debate here and I am opposed to it with a certain lack of support. – I wonder: What's the problem? ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-14-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e19940	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Appreciate Seers and Advisors! At first glance, gaming and sports may seem like insurmountable contrasts, but e-sport shows us that these two worlds in the professional field have more similarities than differences. As in traditional sports, the community and club life are a very important part of the overall, of course, alongside physical and mental fitness. Successful e-sporters do not rarely have reflexes such as a tennis player, the skill of a archer or the martial spirit of a football player or a football player. In addition to the prizes that are now moving in the millions range, there are also strong similarities to traditional sport from a cultural point of view. Because of the growing popularity, e-sport tournaments have now become major events. This year in Katowice, tens of thousands of local spectators have been filmed and millions more have watched the various streaming platforms. So we see that e-sport also moves masses and is enthusiastic, and the trend is rising. However, traditional sport has something that is not yet available to the e-sport: recognition. In order to address this abuse and also to address the issues relating to the subject matter, we have here jointly requested and decided on a working group last year. Since then, stakeholders have produced a report with concrete options for action and opportunities to resolve these issues in more than 300 session hours and spread over seven sub-groups. I am very glad that this working group has not only stopped with the very controversial question of whether e-sport is really sport or not, but has looked at today's conditions in the subgroups and that, as a result, problem solutions have also been presented. The questions raised are: How do we give e-sports agreements legal certainty on tax issues? How do we ensure youth protection? How can more fairness be provided in the competitions? Other topics include doping problems as well as health issues, more participation of women, the demarcation of gambling and sports betting in general. This is a selection of topics in which not only the Ministries, but also with the participation of representatives from traditional sports – such as the Sport Austria, the Askoe, the Sports Union – was worked on, the National Anti-Doping Agency Nada and, of course, the E-Sport Association of Austria. I quote a part of the report on tax matters: ‘In the case of E-Sports, applications for community benefit are currently decided on a case-by-case basis. Often general information and precedents on e-sport are missing from the responsible financial official. Therefore, there is no clear picture throughout Austria [...]. As a result of the working group, it is recommended that e-sport should be recognised as a non-profit purpose, provided that an association also fulfils all the other points required by tax law.’ – This is, for example, an issue that we should address and address at the federal level. In the field of youth protection, the E-Sports Associations want to set a good example and have developed, among other things, a Youth Protection Code to apply to events and competitions. A central issue here is compliance with the age recommendation for the games used. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] The topic of lootboxes has also been edited. Among other things, in order to be able to draw a clear definition of gambling, it is recommended that video games in which gambling-like elements occur are labeled. These and other recommendations provide a good basis for further steps that we now have to take as a policy at federal and national level. If we manage to remain as committed and constructive as the various stakeholders within the framework of the working group, which have quite different positions on the e-sport, we are on the best way to pay the e-sport the recognition he deserves. A few more words to the colleague Steger: One can also deal forever with the question of whether sport and e-sport are equal or not. The reality is: We already have athletes participating in international competitions, and for these we ask ourselves questions for which we need to work out solutions. However, we can also continue to discuss whether e-sports should be recognized as sports. I would like once again to thank all the organisations involved and representatives of traditional sport and the E-sports association, including President Stefan Baloh. The working groups were characterized by mutual appreciation and exchange of experience. The aim now is to analyse the results of the report in detail and to bring solutions to implementation. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-14-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e20686	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The fact that there is short-time work has come to a broad public, especially in times of the Covid-19 crisis, when it was used as a measure to secure employment, has become so well aware. At record times we had up to 1.3 million people in short work. In fact, however, short work has been an instrument that has been for a very long time, an instrument that has been applied even before the Covid-19 crisis. It is an instrument to help companies bridge short-term economic disturbances. What's the goal? – Workers should be kept in operation as best they can – working time will be reduced short, temporary, and income replacement from the AMS – so that the operation can rise as quickly as possible when the crisis is over, can increase working time as quickly as possible, and workers can return to full employment as quickly as possible. This has great advantages for both the workforce and companies: The workforce remains largely employed by its employer and remains employed there. It cannot bring its skills, its potential, into unemployment, has higher incomes, and above all has a perspective in this business, so it must not look for a new job. For the employer, for the company, short-time work again has the very great advantage that the company can keep employees who are experienced in the business processes that are familiar with production, in operation, and can use them again if necessary. It is not necessary to seek, recruit and learn new employees, and can therefore raise production at once again. In other words, short work is an important labour market policy and economic policy instrument, which, as already mentioned, has always existed, and now nothing more happens than that we come back to a model of short-term work, which is no longer the Covid-19 short-term work model. Why? – Because this model has been tailored to a very specific economic situation, to lockdowns where companies have completely reduced their services, their production, because measures were needed, and partly even reduced working time to 0 percent. That's not the way it is now. We no longer need this instrument, this Covid-19 short work, and that is why the new model is one that actually addresses immediate economic crises – now in connection with supply chain problems, with the Ukraine war, with Putin's attack on Ukraine or with other global uncertainties. In other words, the access is not so easy with Weitem, the access is not so easy anymore. It also provides for a three-week consultation period for companies in which it is also seen whether there are alternatives. Working time may be reduced only to 50 percent, no longer to 0 percent. As I said, the figures speak a rather clear language: Of 1.3 million or hundreds of thousands in short-time work, no longer can be a speech, current, I believe, 26 000 people are registered in short-time work, and these are mainly people in industry. That is to say, precisely these crises are reflected because of the economic and political problems that have arisen globally, among other things, with the war in Ukraine, or the supply chain problems there. That is why we need this short-term work, which is why it is important to extend this short-term work, and therefore, of course, we are also quite clear that this short-term work will be decided accordingly, this follow-up model to the Covid-19 model, which is quite appropriate to the crises that can be expected, so that employees can retain their jobs and businesses. Thank you.

Austria is also committed to this and has therefore adopted the sales regulation. This is, in our view, a top priority because we European neighbours have special responsibility. We are already doing a lot: the organization of quarters by the BMI in close cooperation with the federal states, the municipalities and the aid organizations – as happens in the communities Incredible, thanks to the many people who provide their homes to the displaced –, unbureaucratic, rapid and uncomplicated admission of Ukrainian displaced persons and access to health insurance, labour market, education and social assistance. To return to the application: We also create the legal basis for further development of the so-called Temporary Protection Platform. This platform allows the direct exchange of information between EU Member States to ensure the rights of displaced people from Ukraine. This will ensure the rights of the vulnerable in all Member States. Austria's full solidarity applies to Ukraine, which is currently victims of an unprecedented military aggression. For us, it is a matter of course that we alleviate the suffering of displaced people from Ukraine in terms of neighborhood aid. Thank you.
